Kodaira vanishing theorems for Kähler Lie algebroids
Tengzhou Hu

Abstract
A Lie algebroid is a generalization of Lie algebra that provides a general framework to describe the symmetries of a manifold. In this paper, we generalize the Kodaira vanishing theorem, which is a basic result in complex geometry, to Kähler Lie algebroids. The generalization of the Kodaira vanishing theorem states that the kernel of the Lie algebroid Laplace operator on Lie algebroid positive line bundle-valued ( p , q ) (p,q) -forms vanishes when p + q p+q is sufficiently large. The most difficult part of the proof of the generalized Kodaira vanishing theorem is the generalization of the Kähler identities to Lie algebroids. In this paper, we provide an approach by using local coordinate calculation.

1
Introduction

The notion of groupoid, first introduced by Brandt in 1926, refers to a small category in which every morphism is invertible. A groupoid is very much like a group except that the product is only partially defined. As a groupoid can be thought of as a "many-object generalization" of a group, a similar object such as a many-object generalization of a Lie group is called a Lie groupoid. Lie groupoids were introduced by Charles Ehresmann as "differentiable groupoids"
Ehresmann 1959
;
Ehresmann 1963
in 1959. A Lie groupoid can also be understood as a groupoid equipped with a smooth differential structure.

Recall that Lie algebra is associated with a Lie group as the associated infinitesimal object. Similarly, the infinitesimal object associated with a Lie groupoid is called a Lie algebroid. This concept was first introduced by Jean Pradines
Pradines 1963
as the infinitesimal counterpart of Ehresmann’s differentiable groupoids in 1967. It was also independently introduced by Lichnerowicz in the study of Poisson manifolds
Lichnerowicz 1977
in 1977. In the later decades, Lie algebroids appeared in the study of many geometric areas. In Poisson geometry, Weinstein, Coste, and Dazord proved that the Lie algebroid of a symplectic groupoid is the cotangent bundle of the base manifold equipped with a bracket of 1-forms
A. Coste 1987
in 1987. This demonstrated that there is a Lie algebroid structure on the cotangent bundle for any Poisson manifold. The books written by Mackenzie
Mackenzie 1987
;
Mackenzie 2005
are a good reference for Lie algebroids in the theory of group actions and connection theory. The study of Lie algebroids in foliation theory can be seen in
Moerdijk 2005
.

After decades of development, a Lie algebroid has been defined as a triple consisting of a vector bundle
\mathfrak{g}\to M
, a Lie bracket
[\,,\,]
on the space of sections, and a morphism of the vector bundle
\rho:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathrm{T}M
preserving the Lie bracket. A natural example of Lie algebroids is
\mathfrak{g}=\mathrm{T}M
. Thus, we can view a Lie algebroid as a generalization of a tangent bundle. Then, it is natural to generalize notations including differentials, differential forms, connections, and curvatures to Lie algebroids through the same algebraic formal definitions. Analogous to a complex manifold, Chemla defined a complex Lie algebroid on the sheaf of a local section
Chemla 1999
in 1999. Instead of using the language of sheaf theory to define complex Lie algebroids, we use the integrable almost complex structure from Weinstein’s definition of a complex Lie algebroid
A. Cannas da Silva 1999
.

In this paper, the main object we focus on is the Kähler Lie algebroid. The concept of a Kähler manifold was introduced by Kähler in 1933, where he defined a complex manifold that carries a Hermitian metric which is locally given by
\sqrt{-1}\partial\,\bar{\partial}\phi
for a pluri-subharmonic function
\phi
. This original definition is close to the current definition of a Kähler manifold. Through the viewpoint of complex geometry, the Kähler manifold is a complex manifold with a Hermitian metric whose associated 2-form is closed. This 2-form is called the Kähler form. Its cohomology class is topological invariant, meaning that it is independent of the metric. Later, people found that this class also represents the first Chern class of the manifold. An important basic result associated with a Kähler form is the Kähler identities, which are a collection of identities between operators on a Kähler manifold that relate
\bar{\partial}
to their adjoints, contractions, and wedge operators of the Kähler form. It was first proven by Hodge and appeared in his book on harmonic integrals in 1941. The Kähler identities, combined with Hodge theory, can produce many results in algebraic geometry. Based on the Kähler identities, the Bochner–Kodaira–Nakano identity shows the relation between the antiholomorphic Laplacian of a vector bundle over a Kähler manifold with the Kähler form and the torsion of the Hermitian metric. Following the Bochner–Kodaira–Nakano identity and Hodge theory, Kodaira proved the famous Kodaira vanishing theorem, which is a basic result in complex geometry and algebraic geometry that describes the cohomology group of positive line bundles over a Kähler manifold when its degree is sufficiently large.

Kähler Lie algebroids have not been completely studied. Some mathematicians studied the para-Kähler structures on Lie algebroids
E. Leichtnam 2007
;
S. Benayadi 2016
;
E. Peyghan 2018
. Benayadi and Boucettaa studied exact para-Kähler Lie algebroids as a generalization of exact para-Kähler Lie algebras
S. Benayadi 2016
, which leads to a natural generalization of pseudo-Hessian manifolds. Leichtnam, Tang, and Weinstein introduced the notion of a para-Kähler structure on a complex Lie algebroid in their study of Poisson geometry and deformation quantization near a strictly pseudoconvex boundary
E. Leichtnam 2007
. Peyghan, Nourmohammadifar, and Makhlouf introduced and studied para-Kähler hom-Lie algebroids
E. Peyghan 2018
. Some mathematicians also tried to generalize the Kodaira vanishing theorem on Lie algebroids. In
Z. Pirbodaghi 2019
;
Z. Pirbodaghi 2020
, Pirbodaghi and Rezaii defined and studied Hermitian and Kähler structures on transitive Lie algebroids, which are Lie algebroids whose anchor maps are surjective. They defined the integration of top degree Lie algebroid by introducing an "inner operator" which maps Lie algebroid forms to differential forms. They then generalized the notations from complex manifolds to complex Lie algebroids. Using the generalized Lefschetz decomposition to avoid local coordinate calculations, they obtained generalized Hodge identities on transitive Lie algebroids. At the end of their paper, they presented a generalized Kodaira vanishing theorem on transitive Lie algebroids. The theorem states that if a line bundle is Lie algebroid positive (they did not define positive in their thesis, but the statement is a generalization of a positive line bundle as it requires the first Lie algebroid Chern class to be "positive"), then the space of Lie algebroid harmonic
(p,q)
-forms is trivial when
p+q>n
, where
n
is the complex dimension of the transitive Lie algebroid.

In this paper, we study and give a proof of the generalization of Kähler identities on Lie algebroids without any restriction on anchor maps. The proof in our work is based on a local unitary frame. However, since Hodge theory on Lie algebroids is not fully developed, our generalization of the Kodaira vanishing theorem is not as strong as the classical Kodaira vanishing theorem. The vanishing object in our theorem is the kernel of the Laplace operator. The theorem is stated as follows.

Theorem

(Lie algebroid Kodaira–Nakano vanishing theorem)
.

Let
(M,\mathfrak{g},\omega)
be a Kähler Lie algebroid and
E\to M
be a positive line bundle. The kernel of

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}:\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)\to\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)

vanishes when
p+q>n
. The same result holds on a negative line bundle under the condition that
p+q<n
.

This paper is organized as follows.

•

The main topic of Section
2
is Kähler Lie algebroids. After giving the definition of Kähler Lie algebroids, we generalize notations on manifolds, like differential, connection and formal adjoint, to Lie algebroid. Then, we will define the sign of permutation together with some basic formulas which will be repeatedly used in the calculations of the Lie algebroid Kähler identities. The rest of the section presents the Lie bracket and the Lie algebroid differential in terms of a local frame, thereby generating four crucial coefficients. The condition of the closedness of the Lie algebroid Kähler form produces two constraints on these coefficients, which play an essential role in the proof of the Lie algebroid Kähler identities.

•

Section
3
aims to present a generalized Kodaira vanishing theorem for Lie algebroids. It covers the Lie algebroid Kähler identities, the Lie algebroid Bochner–Kodaira–Nakano identity and the Lie algebroid Kodaira vanishing theorem. Since the proof of the Lie algebroid Kähler identities is very long, we put the corresponding calculations into the appendices. At the end of this section, we introduce a Kähler b-manifold as an example of a Kähler Lie algebroid. We explain the equivalence of the notations in the b-category and the Lie algebroid category. We then rewrite the vanishing theorem under the terminologies in the b-category. Finally, we show an example of a Kähler b-manifold.

2
Preliminaries

2.1
Kähler Lie algebroid

To discuss the complex structure of a Lie algebroid, we mainly follow the definition of a complex Lie algebroid by Weinstein in
Weinstein 2006
and associate this definition with the notion of almost complex Lie algebroids over almost complex manifolds
C. Ida 2016
. The almost complex structure of a Lie algebroid is effectively the natural extension of an almost complex manifold when defining an endomorphism
J
of the Lie algebroid. This is integrable if its Nijenhuis tensor of
J
is zero.

Definition 1
.

A
complex Lie algebroid

\mathfrak{g}\to M
is a complex vector bundle over a smooth manifold
M
with the following structures:

1.

A
Lie bracket

[\bullet,\bullet]:\Gamma(M,\mathfrak{g})\times\Gamma(M,\mathfrak{g})\to\Gamma(M,\mathfrak{g})
, where
\Gamma(M,\mathfrak{g})
is the space of sections of
\mathfrak{g}
. We can naturally extend the Lie bracket to sections of
\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}
by linearity.

2.

A vector bundle morphism
\rho:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathrm{T}M^{\mathbb{C}}
which is called an
anchor map
.

3.

Leibniz rule

[v_{1},fv_{2}]=f[v_{1},v_{2}]+(\rho(v_{1})f)v_{2}
for all
v_{1},v_{2}\in\Gamma(M,\mathfrak{g})
and
f\in C^{\infty}(M)
, where
\rho(v_{1})f
is the action of a vector field on a smooth function.

4.

The almost complex structure
J:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}
from the complex vector bundle structure is integrable. That is saying the Nijenhuis tensor

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(\cdot,\cdot):\Gamma(M,\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}})\times\Gamma(M,\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}})\to\mathbb{C}

such that

\mathcal{N}(v_{1},v_{2}):=[J\,v_{1},J\,v_{2}]-J[J\,v_{1},v_{2}]-J[v_{1},J\,v_{2}]-[v_{1},v_{2}]
vanishes.

We use
\mathfrak{g}
to denote a complex Lie algebroid. Lie algebroids in this paper are all complex Lie algebroids unless specified otherwise. Analogous to the decomposition of the complexified tangent bundle
\mathrm{T}M^{\mathbb{C}}
on a complex manifold, we decompose the complexified Lie algebroid into

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}:=\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}=\mathfrak{g}^{1,0}\oplus\mathfrak{g}^{0,1},

where
\mathfrak{g}^{1,0}
is the eigenspace of
J
for the eigenvalue
\sqrt{-1}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{0,1}
is the eigenspace of
J
for the eigenvalue
-\sqrt{-1}
.

Recall that the Kähler manifold is a complex manifold with a Hermitian metric and the corresponding Kähler form. The smoothly varying positive-definite Hermitian form on each fiber determined by the Hermitian metric is called the
canonical
(1,1)
-form
, which is called the Kähler form if it is closed. For the generalization of the Kähler manifold to Lie algebroids, let us observe a complex Lie algebroid
\mathfrak{g}
with a Hermitian metric

\displaystyle h:\mathfrak{g}^{1,0}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{0,1}\to\mathbb{C}

which determines a smoothly varying positive-definite Hermitian Lie algebroid form on each fiber. We call this form
the canonical
(1,1)
-Lie algebroid form
(again, corresponding to the Hermitian metric).

Definition 2
.

A
Kähler Lie algebroid

(\mathfrak{g},M,\rho)
is a complex Lie algebroid carrying a Hermitian metric on
\mathfrak{g}
whose associated canonical
(1,1)
-Lie algebroid form
\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{1,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
is closed. We call
\omega
a
Kähler Lie algebroid form
.

On an open chart
U
of
M
, the unitary frame
\{\xi_{i}\}
spans
{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}^{1,0}|_{U}
with respect to the Hermitian metric, where the length of this frame is
\|\xi_{i}\|_{h}=2
. The following frames for
{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}^{0,1}|_{U}
,
\mathfrak{g}^{1,0}|_{U}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{0,1}|_{U}
are induced by
\{\xi_{i}\}
.

•

\{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\}
spanning
{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}^{0,1}|_{U}
, which is conjugate to
\{\xi_{i}\}
.

•

\{v_{i}\}\in\Gamma(M,\mathfrak{g}^{1,0}|_{U})
is the dual of
\{\xi_{i}\}
, which spans
\mathfrak{g}^{1,0}|_{U}
.

•

\{v^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\}\in\Gamma(M,\mathfrak{g}^{0,1}|_{U})
is the dual of
\{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\}
, which spans
\mathfrak{g}^{0,1}|_{U}
.

The expression of the Kähler Lie algebroid form on an open chart
U
is

\displaystyle\omega|_{U}=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i},

where
\{\xi_{i}\}
and
\{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\}
are the frames of
{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}^{1,0}|_{U}
and
{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}^{0,1}|_{U}
, respectively. This expression is formally the same as the standard Kähler form on
\mathbb{C}^{n}
when replacing
\xi_{i}
with
\mathrm{d}z_{i}
and
\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}
with
\mathrm{d}\bar{z}_{i}
.

Some notations on differential manifolds can be generalized to Kähler Lie algebroid. We define the notations of the exterior algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{k}:=\bigwedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{p,q}:=\bigwedge^{p}\mathfrak{g}^{1,0}\otimes\bigwedge^{q}\mathfrak{g}^{0,1}
. The natural properties of
\otimes
and
\oplus
in the exterior algebra give the equality
\mathfrak{g}^{k}=\bigoplus_{p+q=k}\mathfrak{g}^{p,q}
. A similar construction can be made on the dual of a complex Lie algebroid
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
as long as this gives a complex structure on
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
through the pullback of
J
. By using the decompositions
\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}=\mathfrak{g}^{1,0}\oplus\mathfrak{g}^{0,1}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{*\mathbb{C}}={\mathfrak{g}^{*}}^{1,0}\oplus{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}^{0,1}
, we define the conjugate map

\displaystyle\bar{}:\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle\to\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle v+\sqrt{-1}J(v)

\displaystyle\mapsto v-\sqrt{-1}J(v)

\displaystyle v-\sqrt{-1}J(v)

\displaystyle\mapsto v+\sqrt{-1}J(v)

for
v\in\mathfrak{g}
, and

\displaystyle\bar{}:\mathfrak{g}^{*\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle\to\mathfrak{g}^{*\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle\xi+\sqrt{-1}J(\xi)

\displaystyle\mapsto\xi-\sqrt{-1}J(\xi)

\displaystyle\xi-\sqrt{-1}J(\xi)

\displaystyle\mapsto\xi+\sqrt{-1}J(\xi)

for
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. The definition implies
\bar{v}=v
for any
v\in\mathfrak{g}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}
and
\bar{\xi}=\xi
for any
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*\mathbb{C}}
.

Let
\mathcal{A}^{k}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M):=\Gamma(M,{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}^{k})
be the space of
Lie algebroid
k
-forms
. Then, the Lie algebroid differential is defined as the generalization of the de Rham differential.

Definition 3
.

The Lie algebroid differential
\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}:\mathcal{A}^{k}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\to\mathcal{A}^{k+1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
is defined as

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\phi(v_{1},\cdots,v_{k+1})=\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{i}\rho(v_{i})(\phi(v_{1},\cdots,\widehat{v_{i}},\cdots,v_{k+1}))\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{i<j}(-1)^{i+j-1}\phi([v_{i},v_{j}],v_{1},\cdots,\widehat{v_{i}},\cdots,\widehat{v_{j}},\cdots,v_{k+1})\end{pmatrix}.

The definition of
\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}
is formally same as the definition of the classical de Rham differential, so its application to the exterior product of local frames is formally the same as the case for the classical de Rham. That is

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(f\,\zeta_{i_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\zeta_{i_{k}})=(\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\zeta_{i_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\zeta_{i_{k}}+\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k}(-1)^{j}f\,\zeta_{i_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge(\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\zeta_{i_{j}})\wedge\cdots\wedge\zeta_{i_{k}},

where
\{\zeta_{i}\}
is a local frame, and
f
is a smooth function on
U
. Its definition can also naturally be generalized and applied to the Lie algebroid
(p,q)
-forms
\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M):=\Gamma(M,{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}^{p,q})
. To achieve the definition of
\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}
on any degree of
(p,q)
Lie algebroid differential form, we can check the image of

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}:\mathcal{A}^{1,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\to\mathcal{A}^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)=\mathcal{A}^{2,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\oplus\mathcal{A}^{1,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\oplus\mathcal{A}^{0,2}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)

does not actually contain any components in
\mathcal{A}^{0,2}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
. This is promised since

\displaystyle[v_{1},v_{2}]\subset\Gamma(M,\mathfrak{g}^{1,0})\qquad\text{and}\qquad[v^{{}^{\prime}}_{1},v^{{}^{\prime}}_{2}]\subset\Gamma(M,\mathfrak{g}^{0,1})

for any
v_{1},v_{2}\in\Gamma(M,\mathfrak{g}^{1,0})
and
v^{{}^{\prime}}_{1},v^{{}^{\prime}}_{2}\in\Gamma(M,\mathfrak{g}^{0,1})
since the almost complex structure J is integrable. Then, using the natural projections

\displaystyle\pi_{1}:\mathcal{A}^{2,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\oplus\mathcal{A}^{0,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\to\mathcal{A}^{2,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\pi_{2}:\mathcal{A}^{2,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\oplus\mathcal{A}^{0,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\to\mathcal{A}^{0,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M),

we define
\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}
on
\mathcal{A}^{1,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
by setting

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}:=\pi_{1}\,\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}:\mathcal{A}^{1,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\to\mathcal{A}^{2,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}:=\pi_{2}\,\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}:\mathcal{A}^{1,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\to\mathcal{A}^{1,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M).

Therefore, we can conclude the decomposition of
\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}
on
\mathcal{A}^{1,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}=\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}+\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}:\mathcal{A}^{1,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\to\mathcal{A}^{2,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\oplus\mathcal{A}^{1,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M).

Analogous to the case of
\mathcal{A}^{1,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
, the similar decomposition of the Lie algebroid differential

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}=\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}+\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}:\mathcal{A}^{0,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\to\mathcal{A}^{1,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\oplus\mathcal{A}^{0,2}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)

also holds on
\mathcal{A}^{0,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
. By checking
\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(f\,\xi_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{p}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{q})
, the decomposition
\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}=\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}+\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}
can be generalized to

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}=\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}+\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}:\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\to\mathcal{A}^{p+1,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\oplus\mathcal{A}^{p,q+1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M).

for any
(p,q)
-degree Lie algebroid differential form.

Definition 4
.

Suppose
(\mathfrak{g},M,\rho)
is a Lie algebroid and
E\to M
is a vector bundle. A
Lie algebroid connection
on
E
is a linear operator

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla}:\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)\to\mathcal{A}^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)

satisfying the Leibniz rule

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla_{v}}(f\,s)=f\,\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla_{v}}(s)+f_{*}\big(\rho(v)\big)(s),

where
v\in\Gamma(M,\mathfrak{g}),f\in C^{\infty}(M),s\in\Gamma(M,E)
.

Analogous to the classical connection construction, we can generalize the Lie algebroid connection to the higher order:

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla}:\mathcal{A}^{k}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)\to\mathcal{A}^{k+1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E),

and define its corresponding
Lie algebroid curvature tensor
as

\displaystyle R(\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla}):=\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla}^{2}\in\mathcal{A}^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,\mathrm{End}(E)).

In the classical case of integration on a manifold, a non-vanishing section of
\wedge^{top}\mathrm{T}^{*}M
gives a volume form. However, in the case of a Lie algebroid, any section of
\wedge^{top}\mathfrak{g}^{*}
does not automatically generate a volume form directly. The solution is reached by introducing
transversal densities
, which are sections of
L_{\mathfrak{g}}:=\bigwedge^{top}\mathrm{T}^{*}M\otimes\bigwedge^{top}\mathfrak{g}
.

Definition 5
.

Suppose
\mathfrak{g}\to M
is the Lie algebroid associated with the Lie groupoid
G\rightrightarrows M
. We say the Lie groupoid
G
is unimodular if there exists a global nonvanishing section
\Omega
of
L_{\mathfrak{g}}
.

For any
s\in\Gamma(M,\wedge^{top}\mathfrak{g}^{*})
, its integral over M is

\displaystyle\int_{M}\langle s,\Omega\rangle,

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
pairs the
\wedge^{top}\mathfrak{g}
and
\wedge^{top}\mathfrak{g}^{*}
components and produces
\langle s,\Omega\rangle
, a volume form on M.

Recall that the top degree wedge product of a Kähler form is a canonical volume form. Following this idea, the top degree wedge product of the Lie algebroid Kähler form
\omega^{n}\in\Gamma(M,\bigwedge^{top}\mathfrak{g}^{*})
gives rise to a Lie algebroid volume form since its combination with the transversal density
\Omega\in\Gamma(M,\bigwedge^{top}\mathrm{T}^{*}M\otimes\bigwedge^{top}\mathfrak{g})
produces an actual volume form:

\displaystyle\langle\omega^{n},\Omega\rangle\in\Gamma(M,\bigwedge^{top}\mathrm{T}^{*}M).

We use this canonical Lie algebroid volume form to define the inner product

\displaystyle\big(\xi_{,}\phi\big)_{\mathcal{A}^{k}_{\mathfrak{g}}}:=\int_{M}\langle\big(\xi_{,}\phi\big)_{h}\frac{\omega^{n}}{n!},\Omega\rangle

for
\xi_{,}\phi\in\mathcal{A}^{k}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
, where
\big(,\big)_{h}
is the Hermitian metric on
\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. The inner product leads to the introduction of an adjoint operator. The formal adjoint of a Lie algebroid differential
\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}
is an operator

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}:\mathcal{A}^{k+1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\to\mathcal{A}^{k}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)

satisfying
\big(\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\xi_{,}\phi\big)_{\mathcal{A}^{k}_{\mathfrak{g}}}=\big(\xi_{,}\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\phi\big)_{\mathcal{A}^{k}_{\mathfrak{g}}}
. Similarly,
\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
and
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
are the formal adjoints of
\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}
. Let
\epsilon_{\phi}(\cdot):=\phi\wedge(\cdot)
be the exterior product operator. The contraction operator is
\iota_{\phi}=\epsilon_{\phi}^{*}
.

The Hodge star operator on Lie algebroid differential forms plays a critical role in the proof of the Kähler identities lemma. Our definition is slightly different from the standard reference
P. Griffiths 1994
. This leads to some sign differences in the properties of the Hodge star but does not change the main results of the theory.

Definition 6
.

The Hodge star
\star
is an operator mapping Lie algebroid
(p,q)
-forms to Lie algebroid
(n-q,n-p)
-forms,

\displaystyle\star:\mathscr{A}^{(p,q)}(M)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{A}^{(n-q,n-p)}(M),

such that

\displaystyle\big(\alpha,\beta\big)_{\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}}=\int_{M}\langle\alpha\wedge\overline{\star\beta},\Omega\rangle.

In other words, we require
\alpha\wedge\overline{\star\beta}=\big(\alpha,\beta\big)_{h}\frac{\omega^{n}}{n!}
, which is the exact same as the ordinary definition of the Hodge star on a complex manifold if we omit the extra
\Omega
in the integration. This leads to the local expression of the Hodge star
\star
under the frames
\{\xi\}
and
\{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}\}
, which is formally the same as the classical expression:

\displaystyle\star:f\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\mapsto 2^{p+q-n}f\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{I}^{C}}\wedge\xi_{{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}},

where
\mathbf{I}=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{p}\}
and
\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}=\{j^{{}^{\prime}}_{1},\dots,j^{{}^{\prime}}_{q}\}
are the index sets,
\mathbf{I}^{C}
and
{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}
are their complements in
\{1,\cdots,n\}
and
\{1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\}
, and the sign function gives the sign of permutation from
(1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}})
to
(\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C})
.

In the remaining of this section, we state some properties of the Hodge star and the Lie algebroid differential, which are similar as the cases on differential manifolds.

Proposition 1
.

The following equalities for the Hodge star and the Lie algebroid differential hold:

1.

\overline{\star(f\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})}=\star(\overline{f\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}}).

2.

\overline{\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(f\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})}=\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\overline{(f\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})}.

3.

\star^{2}=(-1)^{n^{2}}(-1)^{p+q}\,\mathrm{id}:\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\to\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
.

4.

(Stoke’s theorem on Lie algebroids)
\int_{M}\langle\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\phi,\Omega\rangle=0
.

5.

-(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\,\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\star
. This leads
\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}=-(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\star
and
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}=-(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\,\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\star
.

Proof.

See Section
6
.
∎

2.2
The Lie bracket and the Lie algebroid differential in terms of a local frame

We will express the Lie bracket and the Lie algebroid differential by the frames
\{\xi_{i}\}
and
\{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\}
in this subsection. Their expression are useful in the proof of the Lie algebroid Hodge identities through local charts.

Firstly, We introduce the function giving the sign of a permutation and the function which sorts the array by size.

Definition 7
.

Suppose
\mathbf{I}=(i_{1},\cdots,i_{n})
is an array consisting of a sequence of numbers and
\tilde{\mathbf{I}}=(\tilde{i}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{i}_{n})
is an array obtained by permuting
\mathbf{I}
. We then define

\displaystyle\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}\\
\tilde{i}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{i}_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\Big):=\text{the sign of the permutation}.

We introduce some propositions for the sign of a permutation which are frequently used in calculations.

Proposition 2
.

1.

Swapping the two rows in a sign of permutation does not change the sign. For example,

\displaystyle\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}\\
\tilde{i}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{i}_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\tilde{i}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{i}_{n}\\
i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\Big).

2.

Suppose
\mathbf{J}
is an array such that
\mathbf{J}\cap(\tilde{i}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{i}_{n})=\emptyset
and
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}\\
\tilde{i}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{i}_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)
is a sign of the permutation. We can insert
\mathbf{J}
in the same position inside the two rows without changing the sign of the permutation:

\displaystyle\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}\\
\tilde{i}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{i}_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{J},i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}\\
\mathbf{J},\tilde{i}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{i}_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}i_{1},\cdots,i_{k},\mathbf{J},i_{k+1},\cdots,i_{n}\\
\tilde{i}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{i}_{k}\mathbf{J},i_{k+1},\cdots,\tilde{i}_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}i_{1},\cdots,i_{n},\mathbf{J}\\
\tilde{i}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{i}_{n},\mathbf{J}\end{smallmatrix}\Big).

3.

Let
\mathbf{I}=(i_{1},\cdots,i_{n})
be an array. Suppose that
\tilde{\mathbf{I}}=(\tilde{i_{1}},\cdots,\tilde{i_{n}})
and
\hat{\mathbf{I}}=(\hat{i_{1}},\cdots,\hat{i_{n}})
are two arrays obtained by permuting
\mathbf{I}
. The following signs of permutations can be merged into one:

\displaystyle\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}\\
\tilde{i_{1}},\cdots,\tilde{i_{n}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}\\
\hat{i_{1}},\cdots,\hat{i_{n}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\hat{i_{1}},\cdots,\hat{i_{n}}\\
\tilde{i_{1}},\cdots,\tilde{i_{n}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big).

In other words, we can merge two signs of permutations by cancelling the rows whose arrays are the same. Furthermore, if a row of a sign of permutation agrees with part of a row in another sign of permutation, we can merge the two signs of permutations into one. For example,

\displaystyle\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{J},i_{1},\cdots,i_{n},K\\
\text{an array by permuting }\mathbf{J},i_{1},\cdots,i_{n},K\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}\\
\tilde{i_{1}},\cdots,\tilde{i_{n}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{J},\tilde{i_{1}},\cdots,\tilde{i_{n}},K\\
\text{an array by permuting }\mathbf{J},i_{1},\cdots,i_{n},K\end{smallmatrix}\Big).

We can also split one sign of permutation into two signs of permutations by reversing the process in the above example:

\displaystyle\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{J},\tilde{i_{1}},\cdots,\tilde{i_{n}},K\\
\text{an array by permuting }\mathbf{J},i_{1},\cdots,i_{n},K\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{J},i_{1},\cdots,i_{n},K\\
\text{an array by permuting }\mathbf{J},i_{1},\cdots,i_{n},K\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}\\
\tilde{i_{1}},\cdots,\tilde{i_{n}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big).

4.

If
\mathbf{I}
and
\mathbf{J}
are two arrays inside the same row of a sign of permutation, then we multiply
(-1)^{|\mathbf{I}||\mathbf{J}|}
after switching
\mathbf{I}
and
\mathbf{J}
. For example,

\displaystyle\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}K,\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J},L\\
\text{a permutation of }K,\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J},L\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=(-1)^{|\mathbf{I}||\mathbf{J}|}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}K,\mathbf{J},\mathbf{I},L\\
\text{a permutation of }K,\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J},L\end{smallmatrix}\Big).

A particular application is that we can simultaneously move two adjacent indices to any position without changing the sign. For example,

\displaystyle\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\alpha,\beta,\mathbf{J}\\
\text{a permutation of }\mathbf{I},\alpha,\beta,\mathbf{J}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha,\beta,\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}\\
\text{a permutation of }\mathbf{I},\alpha,\beta,\mathbf{J}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J},\alpha,\beta,\\
\text{a permutation of }\mathbf{I},\alpha,\beta,\mathbf{J}\end{smallmatrix}\Big).

Then, we define the notation of an ordered array is as follows.

Definition 8
.

Suppose
\mathbf{I}=(i_{1},\cdots,i_{n})
is an array. We define

\displaystyle\mathrm{order}\big(i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}\big):=(\tilde{i}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{i}_{n})

by requiring
\tilde{i}_{1}<\tilde{i}_{2}<\cdots<\tilde{i}_{n-1}<\tilde{i}_{n}
while
\{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}\}=\{\tilde{i}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{i}_{n}\}
.

Now, let
v_{i}\in\Gamma(M,\mathfrak{g}^{1,0}|_{U})
be the dual of
\xi_{i}
and
v^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\in\Gamma(M,\mathfrak{g}^{0,1}|_{U})
be the dual of
\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}
. Then, the Lie bracket of
\mathfrak{g}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle[v_{i},v_{j}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}A^{k}_{i,j}v_{k},

\displaystyle[v_{i},v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}B^{k}_{ij^{{}^{\prime}}}v_{k}+C^{k}_{ij^{{}^{\prime}}}v^{{}^{\prime}}_{k},

\displaystyle[v^{{}^{\prime}}_{i},v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}D^{k}_{ij^{{}^{\prime}}}v^{{}^{\prime}}_{k},

where the coefficients
A^{k}_{ij},B^{k}_{ij}
, and
C^{k}_{ij}
are smooth functions on
U
. These coefficients naturally carry constraints. The first one is

\displaystyle\overline{A^{k}_{ij}}=D^{k}_{ij}

(1)

since

\overline{[v_{i},v_{j}]}=[v^{{}^{\prime}}_{i},v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j}]

. The second one is

\displaystyle\overline{B^{k}_{ij}}=-C^{k}_{ji}

(2)

since
\overline{[v_{i},v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j}]}=[v^{{}^{\prime}}_{i},v_{j}]=-[v_{j},v^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}]
. The coefficients in the local formula of the Lie bracket are also the coefficients of the Lie algebroid cohomology differential. We compute the differential of the frame
\{\xi_{i}\}
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi_{i}(v_{j},v_{k})=\begin{pmatrix}[l]-\rho(v_{j})\big(\xi_{i}(v_{k})\big)+\rho(v_{k})\big(\xi_{i}(v_{j})\big)\\
+\\
\xi_{i}([v_{j},v_{k}])\end{pmatrix}=A^{i}_{jk},

and similarly

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}[l]&\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi_{i}(v_{j},v^{{}^{\prime}}_{k})=B^{i}_{jk},&\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi_{i}(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j},v^{{}^{\prime}}_{k})=0,\\
\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}(v_{j},v_{k})=0,&\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}(v_{j},v^{{}^{\prime}}_{k})=C^{i}_{jk},&\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j},v^{{}^{\prime}}_{k})=D^{i}_{jk}.\end{matrix}

Thus, we summarize the formulas of
\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}
on the frames
\{\xi_{i}\}
and
\{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j}\}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi_{i}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j<k}^{n}A^{i}_{jk}\xi_{j}\wedge\xi_{k}+\sum_{j,k}^{n}B^{i}_{jk}\xi_{j}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k},

(3)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k}^{n}C^{i}_{jk}\xi_{j}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}+\sum_{j<k}^{n}D^{i}_{jk}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k},

(4)

and the formulas of
\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}
on the frames
\{\xi_{i}\}
and
\{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j}\}
as follows:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j<k}^{n}A^{i}_{jk}\xi_{j}\wedge\xi_{k}=\sum_{j,k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{2}A^{i}_{jk}\xi_{j}\wedge\xi_{k}=\sum_{j,k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathrm{order}\big(j,k\big)\\
j,k\end{smallmatrix}\Big)A^{i}_{\mathrm{order}\big(j,k\big)}\xi_{j}\wedge\xi_{k},

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j,k}^{n}B^{i}_{jk}\xi_{j}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k},

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j<k}^{n}D^{i}_{jk}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}=\sum_{j,k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{2}D^{i}_{jk}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}=\sum_{j,k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathrm{order}\big(j,k\big)\\
j,k\end{smallmatrix}\Big)D^{i}_{\mathrm{order}\big(j,k\big)}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k},

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j,k}^{n}C^{i}_{jk}\xi_{j}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k},

where the order function is as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{order}\big(j,k\big)=\begin{cases}(j,k),&j<k,\\
(k,j),&j>k,\\
(j,k)=(k,j),&j=k,\end{cases}

and the sign function is as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}j,k\\
j,k\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=1\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}k,j\\
j,k\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=-1.

The condition that
\omega
is closed applies another constraint on the coefficients
A,B,C
, and
D
through the local formulas of
\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}
.

Lemma 1
.

In a local chart, the canonical
(1,1)
-Lie algebroid form is closed if and only if the constraints

\displaystyle B^{k}_{ij}-B^{j}_{ik}-D^{i}_{jk}=0,\qquad j<k,\qquad\text{and}\qquad A^{i}_{jk}-C^{j}_{ki}+C^{k}_{ji}=0,\qquad j<k,

on the coefficients hold.

Applying the order function and the sign of permutation, we obtain the constraints

\displaystyle B^{k}_{ij}-B^{j}_{ik}=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathrm{order}\big(j,k\big)\\
j,k\end{smallmatrix}\Big)D^{i}_{\mathrm{order}\big(j,k\big)}

(5)

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathrm{order}\big(j,k\big)\\
j,k\end{smallmatrix}\Big)A^{i}_{\mathrm{order}\big(j,k\big)}=C^{j}_{ki}-C^{k}_{ji}

(6)

without considering the order of j and k.

Proof.

The desired identities follow from the following computation of
\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\omega
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\omega

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum_{i}^{n}\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}-\xi_{i}\wedge\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\displaystyle\sum_{j<k}^{n}A^{i}_{jk}\xi_{j}\wedge\xi_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\displaystyle\sum_{k<i}^{n}(B^{i}_{jk}-B^{k}_{ji})\xi_{j}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\\
-\\
\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\displaystyle\sum_{i<j}^{n}(C^{i}_{jk}-C^{j}_{ik})\xi_{i}\wedge\xi_{j}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}\\
-\\
\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\displaystyle\sum_{j<k}^{n}D^{i}_{jk}\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\downarrow(reindex)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\displaystyle\sum_{j<k}^{n}\begin{pmatrix}[l](B^{k}_{ij}-B^{j}_{ik}-D^{i}_{jk})\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}\\
+\\
(A^{i}_{jk}-C^{j}_{ki}+C^{k}_{ji})\xi_{j}\wedge\xi_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\end{pmatrix}.

∎

These two equations are key to the proof of the
Lie algebroid Kähler identities
through local chart. This is not unexpected as we see that the closeness of the classical canonical
(1,1)
-form deduces the classical Kähler identities. Notably, if we use the unitary frame of
\mathrm{T}M
to represent the classical
(1,1)
-form and the Lie bracket, then the corresponding coefficients
A,B,C
, and
D
are in the exact same form discussed above for a Kähler manifold. Thus, we claim that equations

\displaystyle B^{k}_{ij}-B^{j}_{ik}=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathrm{order}\big(j,k\big)\\
j,k\end{smallmatrix}\Big)D^{i}_{\mathrm{order}\big(j,k\big)}

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathrm{order}\big(j,k\big)\\
j,k\end{smallmatrix}\Big)A^{i}_{\mathrm{order}\big(j,k\big)}=C^{j}_{ki}-C^{k}_{ji}

are the sufficient conditions for the Lie algebroid Kähler identities, which are a generalization of the classical Kähler identities.

3
Lie algebroid Kodaira vanishing theorem

The statement of the classical Kodaira vanishing theorem is that for a compact Kähler manifold of complex dimension
n
with a holomorphic vector bundle, the sheaf cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{q}(M,\Omega^{r}(E))
vanishes if
q+r>n
, where
\Omega^{r}(E)
denotes the sheaf of holomorphic
(n,0)
-forms on
M
with values on
E
. Our generalized theorem on the Lie algebroid is slightly weaker than the classical version, which states that the kernel of Lie algebroid
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}
-Laplace operator

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta^{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}:\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)\to\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)

vanishes if
p+q>n
, where
\mathfrak{g}\to M
is a Kähler Lie algebroid and
E\to M
is a Kähler holomorphic vector bundle. We present this theorem in Section
3.3
as the main result of this paper. Many ingredients are required to prove the vanishing theorem. In Section
3.1
, we give the statement of the Lie algebroid Kähler identities, which is the key to the proof of the Lie algebroid Bochner–Kodaira–Nakano identity. The proofs of these identities use several lemmas. Some short lemmas are directly given in Section
3.1
, while those that involve long calculations are given in Section
4
and
5
. In Section
3.2
, we define the Lie algebroid Chern connection and its associated Lie algebroid Laplacians. We then use the Lie algebroid Kähler identities to prove the Lie algebroid Bochner–Kodaira–Nakano identity, which is the key to the proof of the vanishing theorem. In Section
3.3
, we state the generalized vanishing theorem. The Section
3.4
discuss the vanishing theorem on b-manifold, which has a canonical Lie algebroid structure. We define Kähler b-manifold and give an example showing the existence of Kähler Lie algebroid.

3.1
Lie algebroid Kähler identities

The classical Kähler identities on a Kähler manifold are

\displaystyle[\Lambda,\partial]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}{\bar{\partial}}^{*},

\displaystyle[\Lambda,\bar{\partial}]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}{\partial}^{*},

\displaystyle[{\partial}^{*},L]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}\bar{\partial},

\displaystyle[{\bar{\partial}}^{*},L]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}\partial,

where
L
and
\Lambda
are the exterior and interior multiplications on the classical Kähler form. For the proof of the classical Kähler identities, it is enough to prove
[\bar{\partial},\iota_{\omega}]=\sqrt{-1}\partial^{*}
as the remaining three identities automatically hold by taking conjugation and dualization. More details can be found in
Moroianu 2007
;
P. Griffiths 1994
. Our aim is to generalize these identities to the Kähler Lie algebroid. The strategies for the proof of the Lie algebroid Kähler identities are the same, and we only need to check that one equation among these four holds for the Lie algebroid versions of operators, like
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}
,
\iota_{\omega}
, and
\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
. In the proof of the classical Kähler identities on the Kähler manifold, the local expressions of operators such as
\partial
and
\iota_{\omega}
are all written in terms of the frame
\{dz_{i},d\bar{z}_{i}\}
, whose advantages are
\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{i}=0
and
\mathrm{d}^{2}\bar{z}_{i}=0
. Unfortunately, finding the frame
\{\xi_{i}\}
on a Lie algebroid such that
\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{2}\xi=0
may not be possible unless we add extra conditions (e.g., the anchor map is injective) that restrict the generality of our theorem. Therefore, we apply the left-hand side and the right-hand side of the equation on the Lie algebroid form
f\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}
directly and see that the constraints (
5
) and (
6
) are sufficient, which means that the Kähler Lie algebroid naturally leads to the Lie algebroid Kähler identities.

Theorem 1
.

Suppose
(M,\mathfrak{g},\omega)
is a Kähler Lie algebroid, then the following equations

\displaystyle[\Lambda,\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*},

\displaystyle[\Lambda,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}{\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*},

\displaystyle[{\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*},L]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},

\displaystyle[{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*},L]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}

hold, where
L:=\omega\wedge:\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\to\mathcal{A}^{p+1,q+1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
and
\Lambda:=L^{*}:\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)\to\mathcal{A}^{p-1,q-1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
. We call these equations the
Lie algebroid Kähler identities
.

Proof.

Recall that the unitary frame
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
spans
{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}^{1,0}|_{U}
and its dual
\{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
spans
{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}^{0,1}|_{U}
over a small neighborhood
U
. We defined the coefficients
A
,
B
,
C
, and
D
in the local expression of the Lie bracket and obtained two constraints on these coefficients from the condition that
\omega
is closed. Our goal is to use these operators to check that the equation

\displaystyle[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](f\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(f\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

holds for any
(f\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\in\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(U)
, where
\mathbf{I}
and
\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}
are index number sets such that
|\mathbf{I}|=p
and
|\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}|=q
and
f
is a smooth function. We take three steps to complete this check.

1.

Prove

\displaystyle[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j})=\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j}).

(7)

We prove this equation in Lemma
2
.

2.

Assume the equation

[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

holds for the given index number sets
\mathbf{I}=\{i_{1},\cdots,i_{p}\}
and
\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}=\{j^{{}^{\prime}}_{1},\cdots,j^{{}^{\prime}}_{q}\}
. Prove that the equations

\displaystyle[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](\xi_{\alpha}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(\xi_{\alpha}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

(8)

and

\displaystyle[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\alpha}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\alpha}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

(9)

hold. Without loss of generality, we can assume
\alpha<i_{1}
. The proof is complicated and long, so we use Section
4
to present it.

3.

Assume that the equation

[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
holds. Prove

\displaystyle[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](f\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(f\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}),

(10)

where
f
is a smooth function. We prove this equation in Lemma
3
.

∎

Lemma 2
.

[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
.

Proof.

Let us look at the left-hand side for
\xi_{\mathbf{I}},\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}
. The Lie bracket is
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\iota_{\omega}-\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}
, where the first term
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\iota_{\omega}(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j})=0
since

\displaystyle\iota_{\omega}(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j})=\begin{cases}0&i\neq j\\
2\sqrt{-1}&i=j\end{cases}

is a constant function vanishing under
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}
. This leads to the remaining part,
-\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j})
. The expression of
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j})
is

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi_{i})\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}-\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}\xi_{i}\wedge(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{i}_{kl^{{}^{\prime}}}\xi_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}-\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}\xi_{i}\wedge\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathrm{order}\big(l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}\big)\\
l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)D^{j^{{}^{\prime}}}_{\mathrm{order}\big(l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}\big)}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}}.

(11)

We plug
\iota_{\omega}
into the front of (
11
) and obtain

\displaystyle\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{n}\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{h^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{h}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{i}_{kl^{{}^{\prime}}}\xi_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
-\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}\xi_{i}\wedge\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathrm{order}\big(l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}\big)\\
l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)D^{j^{{}^{\prime}}}_{\mathrm{order}\big(l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}\big)}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}.

(12)

Using the contractions
\iota_{\xi_{h}}(\xi_{k})=2\delta_{h,k}
and
\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{h}}(\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k})=2\delta_{h,k}
, we simplify (
12
) as follows:

\displaystyle\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{h^{{}^{\prime}}}}(2B^{i}_{hl^{{}^{\prime}}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})\\
-\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}\displaystyle\sum_{l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathrm{order}\big(l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}\big)\\
l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i^{{}^{\prime}}}}(2D^{j^{{}^{\prime}}}_{\mathrm{order}\big(l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}\big)}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}})\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{-1}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}2B^{i}_{ll^{{}^{\prime}}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
-\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}2B^{i}_{jl^{{}^{\prime}}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
-\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathrm{order}\big(i^{{}^{\prime}},l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}\big)\\
i^{{}^{\prime}},l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)2D^{j^{{}^{\prime}}}_{\mathrm{order}\big(i^{{}^{\prime}},l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}\big)}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathrm{order}\big(l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},i^{{}^{\prime}}\big)\\
l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},i^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)2D^{j^{{}^{\prime}}}_{\mathrm{order}\big(l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},i^{{}^{\prime}}\big)}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}.

We use the constraint
B^{l}_{ji}-B^{i}_{jl}=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathrm{order}\big(i,l\big)\\
i,l\end{smallmatrix}\Big)D^{j}_{\mathrm{order}\big(i,l\big)}
to simplify the term

\displaystyle\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=-2\sqrt{-1}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{i}_{ll^{{}^{\prime}}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
-\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{i}_{jl^{{}^{\prime}}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
-\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathrm{order}\big(i^{{}^{\prime}},l^{{}^{\prime}}\big)\\
i^{{}^{\prime}},l^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)D^{j^{{}^{\prime}}}_{\mathrm{order}\big(i^{{}^{\prime}},l^{{}^{\prime}}\big)}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=-2\sqrt{-1}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{i}_{ll^{{}^{\prime}}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
-\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}(B^{i}_{jl^{{}^{\prime}}}+\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathrm{order}\big(i^{{}^{\prime}},l^{{}^{\prime}}\big)\\
i^{{}^{\prime}},l^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)D^{j^{{}^{\prime}}}_{\mathrm{order}\big(i^{{}^{\prime}},l^{{}^{\prime}}\big)})\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=-2\sqrt{-1}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{i}_{ll^{{}^{\prime}}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}-\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{ji}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}.

We conclude the left-hand side:

\displaystyle[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j})=-2\sqrt{-1}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{i}_{ll^{{}^{\prime}}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}-\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{ji}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}.

(13)

Next, let us look at the right-hand side. Recall that
\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}=-(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\star
, so the right-hand side can be written as

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})=-\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\star(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}).

(14)

Calculating (
14
) step-by-step, we first apply the Hodge star to
(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})
:

\displaystyle\star(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})=2^{2-n}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\wedge\xi_{1^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{n^{{}^{\prime}}}.

(15)

We then apply
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}
to (
15
) and obtain

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\star(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{2-n}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq i}\begin{matrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},c\dots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\dots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n\\
k,1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,\widehat{k},\cdots,n\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}}\wedge\cdots\wedge(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k})\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\wedge\xi_{1^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{n^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{matrix}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}\begin{matrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
l^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\wedge\xi_{1^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}})\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{n^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{matrix}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{2-n}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq i}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n\\
k,1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,\widehat{k},\cdots,n\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k-1}\wedge\begin{pmatrix}[l]\frac{1}{2}D^{k}_{ik}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}\\
+\\
\frac{1}{2}D^{k}_{ki}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\end{pmatrix}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\wedge\\
\xi_{1^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{n^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
l^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\wedge\\
\xi_{1^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{(l-1)^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\begin{pmatrix}[l]B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\\
+\\
B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\end{pmatrix}\wedge\xi_{(l+1)^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{n^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}\end{pmatrix}.

(16)

Looking at the component with
\begin{pmatrix}[l]\frac{1}{2}D^{k}_{ik}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}\\
+\\
\frac{1}{2}D^{k}_{ki}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\end{pmatrix}
, it can be simplified to

\displaystyle\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}k,i\\
\mathrm{order}\big(k,i\big)\end{smallmatrix}\Big)D^{k}_{ki}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}.

For the component with coefficient
B
, we break the sum and rearrange the terms in (
16
):

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\star(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{2-n}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq i}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n\\
k,1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,\widehat{k},\cdots,n\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k-1}\wedge\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}k,i\\
\mathrm{order}\big(k,i\big)\end{smallmatrix}\Big)D^{k}_{ki}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\\
\wedge\xi_{1^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{n^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
l^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\\
\wedge\xi_{1^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{(l-1)^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\wedge\xi_{(l+1)^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{n^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
l^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\\
\wedge\xi_{1^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{(l-1)^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\wedge\xi_{(l+1)^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{n^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{2-n}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq i}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n\\
k,1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,\widehat{k},\cdots,n\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,k-1,k,i,k+1,\cdots,n\\
1,\cdots,n\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}k,i\\
\mathrm{order}\big(k,i\big)\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
D^{k}_{\mathrm{order}\big(k,i\big)}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\wedge\xi_{1^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{n^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
l^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i}\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,(l-1)^{{}^{\prime}},l^{{}^{\prime}},i,(l+1)^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\wedge\xi_{1^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{n^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
l^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i}\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,(l-1)^{{}^{\prime}},j^{{}^{\prime}},i,(l+1)^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\wedge\xi_{1^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{n^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}\end{pmatrix}.

(17)

The next step is applying
\star
to (
17
). Observe that the exterior product of the frames in (
17
), like
\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\wedge\xi_{1^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{n^{{}^{\prime}}}
, is not in the standard form where the holomorphic frames are in front of the anti-holomorphic frames. The expression of the Hodge star applying to this type of local frames is

\displaystyle\star(\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{I}^{C}}\wedge\xi_{{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}})=2^{(|\mathbf{I}^{C}|+|{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}|-n)}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C},\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}},

where
\mathbf{I}
and
\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}
are the arrays of the index numbers. We use this formula and obtain the following expression:

\displaystyle\star\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\star(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{2-n}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq i}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n\\
k,1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,\widehat{k},\cdots,n\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,k-1,k,i,k+1,\cdots,n\\
1,\cdots,n\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}k,i\\
\mathrm{order}\big(k,i\big)\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
D^{k}_{\mathrm{order}\big(k,i\big)}\star(\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\wedge\xi_{1^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{n^{{}^{\prime}}})\end{pmatrix}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
l^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i}\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,(l-1)^{{}^{\prime}},l^{{}^{\prime}},i,(l+1)^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}i}\star(\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\wedge\xi_{1^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{n^{{}^{\prime}}})\end{pmatrix}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
l^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i}\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,(l-1)^{{}^{\prime}},j^{{}^{\prime}},i,(l+1)^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\star(\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\wedge\xi_{1^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{n^{{}^{\prime}}})\end{pmatrix}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{2-n}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq i}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n\\
k,1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,\widehat{k},\cdots,n\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,k-1,k,i,k+1,\cdots,n\\
1,\cdots,n\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}k,i\\
\mathrm{order}\big(k,i\big)\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
D^{k}_{\mathrm{order}\big(k,i\big)}2^{n+n-1-n}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
l^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i}\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,(l-1)^{{}^{\prime}},l^{{}^{\prime}},i,(l+1)^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}i}2^{n+n-1-n}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
l^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i}\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,(l-1)^{{}^{\prime}},j^{{}^{\prime}},i,(l+1)^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}2^{n+n-1-n}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
l^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq i}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n\\
k,1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,\widehat{k},\cdots,n\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,k-1,k,i,k+1,\cdots,n\\
1,\cdots,n\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}k,i\\
\mathrm{order}\big(k,i\big)\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
D^{k}_{\mathrm{order}\big(k,i\big)}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
l^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i}\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,(l-1)^{{}^{\prime}},l^{{}^{\prime}},i,(l+1)^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
i,j^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
l^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,\widehat{i},\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,\widehat{i}\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{j^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,(l-1)^{{}^{\prime}},j^{{}^{\prime}},i,(l+1)^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
l^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,\widehat{l^{{}^{\prime}}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}\end{pmatrix}.

(18)

The signs of permutations in (
18
) can be simplified. The simplification can be found in Section
5.1
. We obtain the simplification of (
18
):

\displaystyle\star\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\star(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})=2\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq i}(-1)^{n^{2}}D^{k}_{\mathrm{order}\big(k,i\big)}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}(-1)^{n^{2}}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}-(-1)^{n^{2}}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}=2(-1)^{n^{2}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq i}D^{k}_{\mathrm{order}\big(k,i\big)}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
-\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}.

(19)

We rewrite

\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}=\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}-B^{j^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}

and

\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}\neq j^{{}^{\prime}}}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}=\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}-B^{j^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}

. Then, (
19
) can be written into

\displaystyle 2(-1)^{n^{2}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}l,i\\
\mathrm{order}\big(l,i\big)\end{smallmatrix}\Big)D^{l}_{\mathrm{order}\big(l,i\big)}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
+\\
\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}-B^{j^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
-\\
(\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}-B^{j^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})\end{pmatrix}=2(-1)^{n^{2}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}l,i\\
\mathrm{order}\big(l,i\big)\end{smallmatrix}\Big)D^{l}_{\mathrm{order}\big(l,i\big)}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
-\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}.

(20)

Using
l
