to replace
l^{{}^{\prime}}
in
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{l^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}
, we further simplify (
20
) and obtain

\displaystyle 2(-1)^{n^{2}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}l,i\\
\mathrm{order}\big(l,i\big)\end{smallmatrix}\Big)D^{l}_{\mathrm{order}\big(l,i\big)}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}B^{l}_{li}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
-\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}=2(-1)^{n^{2}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}l,i\\
\mathrm{order}\big(l,i\big)\end{smallmatrix}\Big)D^{l}_{\mathrm{order}\big(l,i\big)}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}+B^{l}_{li}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
-\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix},

(21)

then we use the constraint for coefficients
B
and
D
to achieve the final simplification of

\star\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\star(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})
:

\displaystyle\star\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\star(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})=2(-1)^{n^{2}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}B^{i}_{ll}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
-\\
\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}.

Recall that
\partial^{*}=-(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\,\bar{\partial}\,\star
. Thus, we can conclude that the right-hand side

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1}\partial^{*}(\xi_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j})=-2\sqrt{-1}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}B^{i}_{ll}\xi_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}-\displaystyle\sum_{l^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}B^{l^{{}^{\prime}}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}i}\xi_{l^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}

is equal to the left-hand side (
13
).

∎

Lemma 3
.

[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](f\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(f\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}).

Proof.

Our proof comprises the following steps:

1.

Calculate
[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](f\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
.

2.

Calculate
\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(f\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
.

3.

Calculate
(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\iota_{\omega}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})-(\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,f)\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}
, which is obtained from Step 1.

4.

Calculate
-\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\bigg((\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\star(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\bigg)
, which is obtained from Step 2.

5.

Assemble the results from Steps 3 and 4 and obtain the expression of
\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(f\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
, then check this is equal to the expression of
[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](f\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
.

Step 1:
Applying
[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}]=\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\iota_{\omega}-\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}
to
(f\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
,

\displaystyle[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](f\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\iota_{\omega}(f\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})-\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}(f\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\begin{pmatrix}[l](\iota_{\omega}f)\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}+f\,\iota_{\omega}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\end{pmatrix}-\iota_{\omega}\begin{pmatrix}[l](\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}+f\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\end{pmatrix},

where
\iota_{\omega}f=0
. Continuing the calculation,

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\begin{pmatrix}[l]f\,\iota_{\omega}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\end{pmatrix}-\iota_{\omega}\begin{pmatrix}[l](\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}+f\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\iota_{\omega}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})+f\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\iota_{\omega}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})-\begin{pmatrix}(\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,f)\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}+f\cdot\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f\,(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\iota_{\omega}-\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}})(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})+(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\iota_{\omega}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})-(\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,f)\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f\,[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})+(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\iota_{\omega}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})-(\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,f)\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}.

(22)

We can separate (
22
) into two parts:

•

The first part is
f\,[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
.

•

The second part is

\displaystyle(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\iota_{\omega}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})-(\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,f)\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}},

(23)

which is equal to a corresponding term in the expression of
\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(f\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
. We return to this later.

Step 2:
Recall that
\partial^{*}=-(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\star
. Thus, we complete the following calculation:

\displaystyle-\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\star(f\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\big(f\,\star(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\bigg((\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\star(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})+f\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\star(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\bigg((\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\star(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\bigg)+\sqrt{-1}f\,\bigg(-(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\star(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\bigg((\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\star(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\bigg)+f\,\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}).

(24)

Summarizing the above computation, we can separate (
22
) into two parts:

•

The first part is
f\,\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
.

•

The second part is

\displaystyle-\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\bigg((\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\star(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\bigg),

(25)

which we check in the next step and is equal to (
23
).

Step 3:
Using
\iota_{\omega}=-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{n}\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{r}}
and the definition of
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}
, we can write

\displaystyle(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\iota_{\omega}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})-(\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,f)\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\displaystyle\sum_{j^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})\wedge(-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2})\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{n}\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{r}}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

(26)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2})\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{n}\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{r}}\big(\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\big).

(27)

To simplify (
26
), we write

\displaystyle\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{r}}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=

\displaystyle\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}}\big(2\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{\prime}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{\prime}\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{\prime}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{\prime}\}}

for
r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}
and
\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{r}}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=0
for
r\in(\mathbf{I}\cup\mathbf{J})^{C}
. We then plug this into (
26
):

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{j^{{}^{\prime}}=1}^{n}\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge(-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}4\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}}.

(28)

The terms such that
j^{{}^{\prime}}\in\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}
are zero due to the repeated frames in the wedge product. We separate the sum into two cases,
j^{{}^{\prime}}\in{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}
and
j^{{}^{\prime}}=r^{{}^{\prime}}
, and rewrite (
28
):

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{j^{{}^{\prime}}\in{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}}\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge(-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}4\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{j^{{}^{\prime}}=r^{{}^{\prime}}}\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge(-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}4\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}[l]-2\sqrt{-1}\displaystyle\sum_{j^{{}^{\prime}}\in{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}}\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\\
+\\
-2\sqrt{-1}\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\end{pmatrix}.

Checking that

•

\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}}=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}\\
\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}
,

•

\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}\\
\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)
,

we conclude that the expression of
(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\iota_{\omega}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
is

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}[l](-2\sqrt{-1})\displaystyle\sum_{j^{{}^{\prime}}\in{{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}}}\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\\
+\\
(-2\sqrt{-1})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\\
r,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}.

(29)

Next, we simplify (
27
). Observing that
\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}=0
when
j^{{}^{\prime}}\in\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}
, we first simplify (
27
) to

\displaystyle(-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2})\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{n}\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{r}}\big(\displaystyle\sum_{j\in{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}}\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\big).

(30)

We then observe that
\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{r}}(\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
is equal to zero except for
r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}
or
r=j\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}
. Thus, (
30
) can be split into

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}[l](-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{r}}\big(\displaystyle\sum_{j\in{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}}\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\big)\\
+\\
(-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{r}}\big(\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\big)\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}[l](-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\displaystyle\sum_{j\in{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}}\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{r}}\big(\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\big)\\
+\\
(-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{r}}\big(\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\big)\end{pmatrix},

(31)

in which

\displaystyle\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{r}}(\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=

\displaystyle\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}}\big(2\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}j^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,j^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}j^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,j^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}j^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r^{{}^{\prime}},j^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}j^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,r^{{}^{\prime}},j^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}}

(32)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}},

(33)

where the number
2
in the first row is obtained from
\iota_{\xi_{r}}(\xi_{r})=2
since
\|\xi_{r}\|_{h}=2
, and

\displaystyle\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{r}}(\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=

\displaystyle\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}}\big(2\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}.

(34)

Plugging (
33
) and (
34
) into (
31
), we conclude that the expression of
(\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,f)\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}
is

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}[l](-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\displaystyle\sum_{j\in{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}}\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,4\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\\
+\\
(-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,(-4)\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\\
r,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}[l](-2\sqrt{-1})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\displaystyle\sum_{j\in{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\\
+\\
(-2\sqrt{-1})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}(-1)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\\
r,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}.

(35)

Finally, we conclude that
(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\iota_{\omega}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})-(\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,f)\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}
is equal to (
29
)
-
(
35
), which is

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}[l](-2\sqrt{-1})\displaystyle\sum_{j^{{}^{\prime}}\in{{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}}}\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\\
+\\
(-2\sqrt{-1})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\\
r,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}[l](-2\sqrt{-1})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\displaystyle\sum_{j\in{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
r,r^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{j^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{r^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\\
+\\
(-2\sqrt{-1})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}(-1)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\\
r,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}.

The cancellation is clear and results in the final simplified expression:

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}[l](-2\sqrt{-1})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\\
r,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
+\\
(-2\sqrt{-1})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}(-1)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\\
r,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-2\sqrt{-1})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\\
r,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}.

(36)

Step 4:
Recalling the definitions of
(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)
and
\star
, we obtain

\displaystyle(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\star(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=\bigg(\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{i^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\bigg)\wedge\bigg(2^{p+q-n}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{I}^{C}}\wedge\xi_{{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}}\bigg).

(37)

It can be observed that the terms such that
i\in\mathbf{I}^{C}
are zero due to the repeated
\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}
in the wedge product of frames. Thus, we simplify (
37
) to

\displaystyle\bigg(\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbf{I}}\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{i^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\bigg)\wedge\bigg(2^{p+q-n}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{I}^{C}}\wedge\xi_{{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbf{I}}2^{p+q-n}\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{i^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{I}^{C}}\wedge\xi_{{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}}.

(38)

We rearrange (
38
) by taking

\displaystyle\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{i}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{I}^{C}}\wedge\xi_{{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}}=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}i,\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\\
\mathrm{order}\big(\mathbf{I}^{C}\cup\{i\}\big),{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{order}\big(\mathbf{I}^{C}\cup\{i\}\big)}\wedge\xi_{{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}},

then the simplified expression of
(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\star(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
is

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbf{I}}2^{p+q-n}\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{i^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}i,\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\\
\mathrm{order}\big(\mathbf{I}^{C}\cup\{i\}\big),{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{order}\big(\mathbf{I}^{C}\cup\{i\}\big)}\wedge\xi_{{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}}.

(39)

Finally, we apply
\star
to (
39
):

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbf{I}}2^{p+q-n}\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{i^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}i,\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\\
\mathrm{order}\big(\mathbf{I}^{C}\cup\{i\}\big),{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\star(\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{order}\big(\mathbf{I}^{C}\cup\{i\}\big)}\wedge\xi_{{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}}),

in which

\displaystyle\star(\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{order}\big(\mathbf{I}^{C}\cup\{i\}\big)}\wedge\xi_{{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}})=

\displaystyle 2^{(n-p+1)+(n-q)-n}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
\mathrm{order}\big(\mathbf{I}^{C}\cup\{i\}\big),{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{n-p-q+1}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
\mathrm{order}\big(\mathbf{I}^{C}\cup\{i\}\big),{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}},

so the expression of
\star\bigg((\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\star(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\bigg)
is

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathbf{J}}2\,\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{i^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}i,\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\\
\mathrm{order}\big(\mathbf{I}^{C}\cup\{i\}\big),{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
\mathrm{order}\big(\mathbf{I}^{C}\cup\{i\}\big),{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\end{pmatrix}\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}.

(40)

We simplify the signs of permutations through

•

\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}i,\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\\
\mathrm{order}\big(\mathbf{I}^{C}\cup\{i\}\big),{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
\mathrm{order}\big(\mathbf{I}^{C}\cup\{i\}\big),{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
i,\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)
,

•

\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
i,\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=(-1)^{(n-p+n-q)(p-1+q)}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
i,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
=(-1)^{(p+q)(p-1+q)}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
i,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
i,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\\
i,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)
,

•

\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)=(-1)^{n^{2}}

and obtain

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}[l]\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1,\cdots,n,1^{{}^{\prime}},1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}}\\
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}i,\mathbf{I}^{C},{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\\
\mathrm{order}\big(\mathbf{I}^{C}\cup\{i\}\big),{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\\
\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}1^{{}^{\prime}},\cdots,n^{{}^{\prime}},1,\cdots,n\\
\mathrm{order}\big(\mathbf{I}^{C}\cup\{i\}\big),{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{C},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\end{pmatrix}=(-1)^{n^{2}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\\
i,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big).

We can now conclude that the expression of
-\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\bigg((\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\star(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\bigg)
is

\displaystyle-\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{n^{2}}\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbf{I}}2\,\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{i^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,(-1)^{n^{2}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\\
i,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sqrt{-1}\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbf{I}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\\
i,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{i^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}.

(41)

Step 5:
Recalling Step 1, the left-hand side
[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](f\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
is equal to

\displaystyle f\,[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})+(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\iota_{\omega}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})-(\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,f)\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}.

The second term in the above expression,
(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\iota_{\omega}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})-(\iota_{\omega}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,f)\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}
, is equal to (
36
). Therefore,

\displaystyle[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](f\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=\begin{pmatrix}[l]f\,[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\\
+\\
(-2\sqrt{-1})\displaystyle\sum_{r\in\mathbf{I}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\\
r,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{r^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{r\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}.

Recalling Step 2, the right-hand side
\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(f\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
is equal to

\displaystyle f\,\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})+(-\sqrt{-1})(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\bigg((\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\star(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\bigg).

The second term in the above expression,
(-\sqrt{-1})(-1)^{n^{2}}\star\bigg((\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}f)\wedge\star(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\bigg)
, is equal to (
41
). Therefore,

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(f\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=\begin{pmatrix}[l]f\,\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\\
+\\
(-2\sqrt{-1})\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbf{I}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\\
i,\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\rho(v^{{}^{\prime}}_{i^{{}^{\prime}}})(f)\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{i\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\end{pmatrix}.

We complete the proof by observing that the left-hand side and the right-hand side are the same.

∎

3.2
Lie algebroid Bochner–Kodaira–Nakano identity

In classical differential geometry, a holomorphic vector bundle over a complex manifold is a vector bundle compatible with the complex structure of the base manifold. That is to say, all entries of the transition matrices are holomorphic functions. Furthermore, recall that a holomorphic vector bundle equipped with a Hermitian metric is called a Hermitian vector bundle. We generalize these concepts to the Kähler Lie algebroid.

Definition 9
.

For a Kähler Lie algebroid
\mathfrak{g}\to M
, we say a complex vector bundle
E\to M
is a
Lie algebroid holomorphic vector bundle
if its transition matrices

\displaystyle\{\phi_{\alpha,\beta}:\mathbb{C}^{\mathrm{rank}_{\mathbb{C}}(E)}\to\mathbb{C}^{\mathrm{rank}_{\mathbb{C}}(E)}\}

associated with an open covering of
M
are Lie algebroid holomorphic. That is to say,
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\phi_{\alpha,\beta})_{i,j}=0
for all entries of the transition matrix. We say a complex vector bundle is a
Lie algebroid Hermitian vector bundle
if it is equipped with a Hermitian metric.

As the entries of the transition matrix are all Lie algebroid holomorphic, the operator
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}
can act on
E
-valued differential forms. That is to say,

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}:\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)\to\mathcal{A}^{p,q+1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)

is well defined when
E
is a Lie algebroid holomorphic vector bundle.

Next, we generalize the Chern connection. Before giving its formal definition, we define the splitting of a connection.

Definition 10
.

Suppose that

\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla}:\Gamma(M,E)\to\mathcal{A}^{1,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)\oplus\mathcal{A}^{0,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)
, then we define the holomorphic part as

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}:\Gamma(M,E)\to\mathcal{A}^{1,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)

and the anti-holomorphic part as

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}:\Gamma(M,E)\to\mathcal{A}^{0,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E).

Using the splitting
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla}=\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}+\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}
, we define the Lie algebroid Chern connection.

Definition 11

(Lie algebroid Chern connection)
.

For a Lie algebroid Hermitian vector bundle
E
, a Lie algebroid Chern connection on
E
is a Lie algebroid connection

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla}:\Gamma(M,E)\to\mathcal{A}^{1,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)\oplus\mathcal{A}^{0,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E),

which is compatible with the Hermitian metric, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle(\gamma)=\langle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla_{\gamma}}\alpha,\beta\rangle+\langle\alpha,\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla_{\gamma}}\beta\rangle

for any
\alpha,\beta,\gamma\in\Gamma(M,E)
, and its anti-holomorphic part
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}=\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}:\Gamma(M,E)\to\mathcal{A}^{0,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)
.

Proposition 3
.

There exists a unique Lie algebroid Chern connection for a Lie algebroid Hermitian vector bundle.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of the existence and uniqueness of the classical Chern connection. Suppose
\{e_{i}\}
is a Lie algebroid holomorphic frame for
E
and set
h_{ij}=\langle e_{i},e_{j}\rangle
. Using the holomorphic frame, we consider the
\mathcal{A}^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
-valued matrix
(\theta_{ij})
such that
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla}(e_{i})=\sum_{j}\theta_{ij}e_{j}
. Observing that
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla}(e_{i})\in\mathcal{A}^{1,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)
since
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}(e_{i})=0
, then

\displaystyle(\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}+\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}})h_{i,j}=\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}h_{i,j}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\langle e_{i},e_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla}e_{i},e_{j}\rangle+\langle e_{i},\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla}e_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\sum_{k}\theta_{ik}e_{k},e_{j}\rangle+\langle e_{i},\sum_{k}\theta_{jk}e_{k}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k}\theta_{ik}h_{kj}+\sum_{k}\overline{\theta_{jk}}h_{ik},

where
\theta_{ik}h_{kj}\in\mathcal{A}^{1,0}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
and
\overline{\theta_{jk}}h_{ik}\in\mathcal{A}^{0,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
. This illustrates the relation between the Hermitian metric matrix
(h_{ij})
and the structure matrix
(\theta_{ij})
for
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla}
such that the Chern connection is uniquely determined by the Hermitian metric. Moreover, the type
(1,0)
-part
\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}h_{i,j}=\sum_{k}\theta_{ik}h_{kj}
in the equation gives
\theta=\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}h\cdot h^{-1}
.
∎

The curvature tensor
R(\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla})=\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla}^{2}
for the Lie algebroid Chern connection is
(1,1)
-type since
(\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}})^{2}=\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{2}=0
and the curvature matrix
\Theta^{2,0}=-(\overline{(\Theta)^{0,2}})^{tr}=0
by the property that the connection matrix
\theta
is skew-Hermitian. Thus, the expansion of the curvature tensor in terms of
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}
and
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}
is

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}\,\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}+\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}\,\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}.

We can further simplify the notations on the right-hand side using the superbracket
[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}},\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}]
. This involves the concept called superbundle
N. Berline 2004
.

Definition 12
.

A
superspace

V
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded vector space
V=V^{+}\oplus V^{-}
. A
superbundle

\mathcal{E}\to M
is a vector bundle
\mathcal{E}\to M
admitting a splitting
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{+}\oplus\mathcal{E}^{-}
such that
\mathcal{E}_{x}=\mathcal{E}^{+}_{x}\oplus\mathcal{E}^{-}_{x}
is a superspace for all
x\in M
.

An operator
D
is called even (degree
(D)=0
) if it preserves the degree of the element in
\mathcal{E}
:

\displaystyle D:\mathcal{E}^{+}\to\mathcal{E}^{+}\qquad\text{and}\qquad D:\mathcal{E}^{-}\to\mathcal{E}^{-},

and is called odd (degree
(D)=1
) if it swamps the degree of the elements in
\mathcal{E}
:

\displaystyle D:\mathcal{E}^{+}\to\mathcal{E}^{-}\qquad\text{and}\qquad D:\mathcal{E}^{+}\to\mathcal{E}^{-}.

With the degree of superoperators, the
superbracket
is defined by

\displaystyle[D_{1},D_{2}]=D_{1}D_{2}-(-1)^{|(D_{1})\cdot(D_{2})}D_{2}D_{1}.

Looking at the space of
E
-valued Lie algebroid forms
\mathcal{E}=\bigoplus_{p,q}\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)
, we define the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
grading

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{+}=\bigoplus_{\text{p+q are even}}\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathcal{E}^{-}=\bigoplus_{\text{p+q are odd}}\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E).

Following the definitions, we can see that
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla}
,
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}
, and
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}
are all odd-degree operators. Consequently, the superbracket between
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}
and
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}
yields

\displaystyle R(\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla})=\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}\,\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}-(-1)^{(-1)(-1)}\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}\,\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}=[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}},\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}].

Let us define and generalize some notations and equations that help us achieve the aim: exploring a generalization of the Bochner–Kodaira–Nakano identity on the Lie algebroid. The first one is
Lie algebroid Laplacians
induced by the Lie algebroid Chern connection:

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta^{1,0}}

\displaystyle:={\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}}^{*}\,\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}+\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}\,{\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}}^{*}=[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}},{\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}}^{*}]

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta^{0,1}}

\displaystyle:={\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}}^{*}\,\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}+\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}\,{\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}}^{*}=\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}.

We can use the notation
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}
-Laplacian
or
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}
for
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta^{0,1}}
. Then, let us generalize the exterior and interior multiplications of the Lie algebroid Kähler form. In Section
3.1
, we have already defined
L
and
\Lambda
on the space of Lie algebroid forms in Theorem
1
. We generalize these to the bundle-valued Lie algebroid forms,

\displaystyle L:=\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{A}^{p+1,q+1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)

\displaystyle\phi\otimes e

\displaystyle\mapsto\omega\wedge\phi\otimes e,

and the formal adjoint of
L
,

\displaystyle\Lambda:=L^{*}:\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)\to\mathcal{A}^{p-1,q-1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E),

on the space of
E
-valued Lie algebroid differential forms. Another generalization concerns the Lie algebroid Kähler identities on bundle-valued
(p,q)
-forms.

Proposition 4
.

For a Kähler Lie algebroid
\mathfrak{g}\to M
and a Lie algebroid Hermitian vector bundle
E\to M
, the Lie algebroid Kähler identities on
E
-valued
(p,q)
-forms are:

\displaystyle[\Lambda,\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}{\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}}^{*},

\displaystyle[\Lambda,\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}{\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}}^{*},

\displaystyle[{\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}}^{*},L]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}},

\displaystyle[{\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}}^{*},L]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}.

Proof.

We can pick a local normal frame on
E
such that the connection
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}=\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\phi
, where
\phi
vanishes at the origin of the local coordinate. Then, we use
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}
and
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}
to replace
\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}
in the Lie algebroid Kähler identities in Theorem
1
.
∎

Using all the ingredients we have defined and constructed, we can obtain the Lie algebroid version of the Bochner–Kodaira–Nakano identity, which formally agrees with the identity on a complex manifold (e.g.,
P. Griffiths 1994
), and the proof is similar as well.

Lemma 4
.

For a Kähler Lie algebroid, the equation

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}=\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta^{1,0}}+\sqrt{-1}[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{2}},\Lambda]

(42)

holds and is called the
Lie algebroid Bochner–Kodaira–Nakano identity
.

Proof.

Following the definition of
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}
,

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle=[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}]

\displaystyle=[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}},{\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}}^{*}].

We use the identity
[\Lambda,\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}]=\sqrt{-1}{\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}}^{*}
and plug it into the expansion of the above Laplacian, obtaining the following formula:

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}=-\sqrt{-1}\big[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}},[\Lambda,\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}]\big].

(43)

We now look at the Jacobi identity:

\displaystyle\big[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}},[\Lambda,\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}]\big]+\big[\Lambda,[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}},\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}]\big]+\big[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}},[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}},\Lambda]\big]=0.

We replace the right-hand side of (
43
) with the last two terms in the Jacobi identity and deduce

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}\big[\Lambda,[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}},\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}]\big]+\sqrt{-1}\big[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}},[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}},\Lambda]\big]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}\big[\Lambda,[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}},\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}]\big]+\sqrt{-1}\big[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}},-\sqrt{-1}{\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}}^{*}\big]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}\big[\Lambda,[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}},\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}]\big]+[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}},{\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}}^{*}],

(44)

where we use the Lie algebroid Kähler identity
[\Lambda,\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}]=\sqrt{-1}{\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}}^{*}
in the second equal sign.

Recalling that
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta^{1,0}}=[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}},{\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}}}^{*}]
and
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{2}}=[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{1,0}},\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{0,1}}]
, we conclude the Lie algebroid Bochner–Kodaira–Nakano identity from (
44
).
∎

3.3
Main theory

Applying the Lie algebroid Bochner–Kodaira–Nakano identity, we can establish the Lie algebroid Kodaira vanishing theorem. The original statement of the theorem on the complex manifold is that the cohomology group of a positive line bundle vanishes when its degree is large enough. For a Lie algebroid, however, the Hodge theory between the space of harmonic E-valued Lie algebroid
(p,q)
-forms, i.e.,
\mathrm{ker}(\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}|_{\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)})
, and the sheaf cohomology of
E
for a Lie algebroid holomorphic vector bundle
E
over
M
, i.e.,
H^{p}(M,\Omega^{q}(E))
, is yet to be developed. Our vanishing theorem is slightly weaker than the classical case and gives some sufficient conditions: the type
(p,q)
such that
p+q
is large enough, which implies the kernel of
\nabla_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}
is trivial. First, let us define the Lie algebroid positive line bundle.

Definition 13
.

A Lie algebroid holomorphic line bundle
E\to M
is
positive
, if there is a hermitian metric on
E
such that the curvature
R\in\mathcal{A}^{1,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
of the Chern connection satisfies that
\sqrt{-1}R
is positive definite. Meanwhile, we say
E\to M
is
negative
if
-\sqrt{-1}R
is positive definite.

Recall that the operator
L
is the exterior multiplication of
\omega
and
\Lambda
is its dual. Both operators are even-degree operators. In the classical case, their commutator
[L,\Lambda]=L\Lambda-\Lambda L
is a scalar multiplication, and this result can be generalized to the Kähler Lie algebroid.

Lemma 5
.

For a Kähler Lie algebroid
(M,\mathfrak{g},\omega)
and the vector bundle
E\to M
, the commutator
[L,\Lambda]
on
\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)
is equal to
(p+q-n)\mathrm{id}
, where
\mathrm{id}:\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)\to\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)
is an identity map.

Proof.

It is enough to verify the identity on a local neighborhood. Recall that locally

\omega=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\xi_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k^{{}^{\prime}}}
. Using the notations that
e_{\xi_{k}}:=\xi_{k}\wedge
and
\iota_{\xi_{k}}:=(e_{\xi_{k}})^{*}
, we can compute the expressions of
L
and
\Lambda
:

\displaystyle L=e_{\omega}=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}e_{\xi_{k}}\,e_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k^{{}^{\prime}}}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Lambda=\iota_{\omega}=-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{k}}.

Then, for any
(p,q)
-Lie algebroid form
\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}
, we can expect that the applications of
L
and
\Lambda
on
\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}
will be similar to the classical case.

We first compute
L\,\Lambda(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
. The following details the calculation:

\displaystyle\Lambda(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{k}}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbf{I}}\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}}(2\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\\
k,\mathbf{I}\backslash{\{k\}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{k\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}4\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\\
k,\mathbf{I}\backslash{\{k\}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I}\backslash{\{k\}},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
k^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash{\{k\}},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash{\{k^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{k\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash{\{k^{{}^{\prime}}\}}}

\displaystyle=-2\sqrt{-1}\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
k,k^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash{\{k\}},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash{\{k^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{k\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash{\{k^{{}^{\prime}}\}}},

\displaystyle L\,\Lambda(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{n}e_{\xi_{h}}\,e_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{h^{{}^{\prime}}}}(-2\sqrt{-1})\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
k,k^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash{\{k\}},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash{\{k^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{k\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash{\{k^{{}^{\prime}}\}}}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\xi_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
k,k^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash{\{k\}},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash{\{k^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{k\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash{\{k^{{}^{\prime}}\}}}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{h\in\mathbf{I}^{C}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}\xi_{h}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{h^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
k,k^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash{\{k\}},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash{\{k^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{k\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash{\{k^{{}^{\prime}}\}}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{h\in\mathbf{I}^{C}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}\xi_{h}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{h^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
k,k^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash{\{k\}},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash{\{k^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{k\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash{\{k^{{}^{\prime}}\}}}\end{pmatrix}.

Next, we compute
\Lambda\,L(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
. The following details the calculation:

\displaystyle L(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbf{I}^{C}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}\xi_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}},

\displaystyle\Lambda\,L(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=

\displaystyle-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{n}\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{h^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{h}}\big(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbf{I}^{C}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}\xi_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{h\in\mathbf{I}^{C}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{h^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{h}}(\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbf{I}^{C}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}\xi_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{h\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\iota_{\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{h^{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\iota_{\xi_{h}}(\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbf{I}^{C}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}\xi_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbf{I}^{C}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}4\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{h\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}4\,\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}k,k^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{h\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
h^{{}^{\prime}},k,k^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{h\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{h^{{}^{\prime}}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}k,k^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\\
h,k,k^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{h\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbf{I}^{C}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}\xi_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{h\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{h^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}[l]\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbf{I}^{C}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}\\
+\\
\displaystyle\sum_{h\in\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}}\mathrm{sgn}\Big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\\
h,h^{{}^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}\backslash\{h\},\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{h^{{}^{\prime}}\}\end{smallmatrix}\Big)\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbf{I}^{C}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}}\xi_{k}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{k^{{}^{\prime}}}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}\backslash\{h\}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}\backslash\{h^{{}^{\prime}}\}}\end{pmatrix}.

From the calculations above, we cancel the repeated terms and obtain
[L,\Lambda]=e_{\omega}\,\iota_{\omega}-\iota_{\omega}\,e_{\omega}=(|\mathbf{I}\cap\mathbf{J}|-|\mathbf{I}^{C}\cap\mathbf{J}^{C}|)\mathrm{\mathrm{id}}=(p+q-n)\mathrm{\mathrm{id}}
.
∎

We are now ready to state and prove the vanishing theorem.

Theorem 2

(Lie algebroid Kodaira–Nakano vanishing theorem)
.

Give a Kähler Lie algebroid
\mathfrak{g}\to M
and a positive line bundle
E\to M
. The kernel of

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}:\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)\to\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)

vanishes when
p+q>n
. The same result holds on a negative line bundle under the change of the condition that
p+q<n
.

Proof.

By the definition of positivity, the curvature of the Lie algebroid Chern connection for
E
is a closed positive
(1,1)
-form. Thus, it determines a Lie algebroid Kähler form
\omega
. Use this Lie algebroid Kähler form in the Lie algebroid Bochner–Kodaira–Nakano identity

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}=\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta^{1,0}}+\sqrt{-1}[\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{2}},\Lambda].

The
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta^{1,0}}
in the equation is a non-negative operator (i.e.,
\langle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta^{1,0}}\phi,\phi\rangle\geq 0,\forall\phi\in\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)
) and
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\nabla^{2}}
is equal to
\sqrt{-1}L
from the definition of a positive line bundle. Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}=\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta^{1,0}}-[L,\Lambda]=\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta^{1,0}}+(p+q-n)\mathrm{\mathrm{id}},

which this implies that
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}
has a trivial kernel if
p+q>n
.
∎

The following corollary will be applied to the kernel of the (Lie algebroid) Dolbeault operator.

Corollary 1
.

Suppose
M\to\mathfrak{g}
is a Kähler Lie algebroid and
E\to M
a positive Lie algebroid holomorphic line bundle. The kernel of

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}:\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)\to\mathcal{A}^{p,q+1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)\oplus\mathcal{A}^{p,q-1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)

vanishes when
p+q>n
. The same result holds on a negative line bundle under the change of the condition that
p+q<n
.

Proof.

The Laplace operator
\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}=\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}=(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})^{2}
, so

\displaystyle\ker(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})\subset\ker(\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}})

is clear. On the other side, if
s\in\ker(\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}})
, then

\displaystyle 0=\big(\prescript{\mathfrak{g}}{}{\Delta_{\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}s,s\big)_{\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle=\big((\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})^{2}s,t\big)_{\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle=\big((\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})s,(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})s\big)_{\mathcal{A}^{p,q+1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)\oplus\mathcal{A}^{p,q-1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,E)}

\displaystyle=||(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})s||^{2},

which implies that
s\in\ker(\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})
as well, thereby completing the proof of Corollary
1
.
∎

3.4
Application of a b-manifold

The notion of b-manifold was introduced by Melrose
Melrose 1993
as the framework for studying differential calculus and differential operators on the manifold with boundary. However, our vanishing theorem is based on manifolds without boundary. Thus, our notation of b-manifold follows
V. Guillemin 2014
;
S. Geudens 2020
, making it a manifold with a distinguished hypersurface.

Definition 14
.

A
b-manifold
is a pair
(M,Z)
consisting of a manifold
M
and a codimension-one submanifold
Z\subset M
. The Serre–Swan theorem assumes that there is a vector bundle
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}\to M
such that the space of its sections is equal to the space of vector fields on
M
that are tangent to
Z
. We denote
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}
the
b-tangent bundle
.

The space of vector fields which are tangent to
Z
is a locally free
C^{\infty}(M)
-module with generators
\{x_{1}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}},\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{2}},\cdots,\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{n}}\}
in a coordinate chart
(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})
that is adapted to
Z=\{x_{1}=0\}
. The natural inclusion
\Gamma(M,\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M})\xhookrightarrow{}\Gamma(M,TM)
induces a vector bundle morphism
\rho:\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}\to\mathrm{T}M
, which is an isomorphism on
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}|_{M\backslash Z}
and an epimorphism on
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}|_{Z}
. Using the vector bundle morphism
\rho
, we can construct a local frame
X_{1},\cdots,X_{n}
of
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}
near the coordinate chart adapted to
Z
which is determined by

\displaystyle\rho(X_{1})=x_{1}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}\qquad\rho(X_{2})=\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{2}}\qquad\cdots\qquad\rho(X_{n})=\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{n}}.

(45)

The space of sections of
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}
is also equipped with a Lie bracket inheriting from the Lie bracket of vector fields on
M
. To describe it more precisely, the Lie bracket on
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}|_{M\backslash Z}
is entirely determined by the Lie bracket of vector fields on
\mathrm{T}M|_{M\backslash Z}
through the isomorphism
\rho:\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}|_{M\backslash Z}\to\mathrm{T}M|_{M\backslash Z}
, and the neighborhood containing
Z
is determined through (
45
). Then,
(\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M},\rho,[,])
forms a Lie algebroid.

The complex b-manifold is introduced by Mendoza
Mendoza 2013
. Its definition is as follows.

Definition 15
.

Let
(M,Z)
be a b-manifold of even real dimension. An
almost CR b-structure
on b-manifold is a subbundle
\mathcal{W}
of the complexification
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M^{\mathbb{C}}}
such that
\mathcal{W}\cap\overline{\mathcal{W}}=0
, where
\mathcal{W}
is the conjugation of
\mathcal{W}
. The "almost" can be dropped if
\overline{\mathcal{W}}
is involutive, i.e.,
[s_{1},s_{2}]\subset\Gamma(M,\overline{\mathcal{W}})
for any
s_{1},s_{2}\in\Gamma(M,\overline{\mathcal{W}})
.

An involutive almost CR b-structure on
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}
is equivalent to the existence of an almost complex structure
J
on
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}
whose Nijenhuis tensor vanishes. Therefore, the b-tangent bundle
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}
is a complex Lie algebroid. The vector bundle morphism
\rho:\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}\to\mathrm{T}M
can be extended to an anchor map
\rho:\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}\to\mathrm{T}M^{\mathbb{C}}
through the natural inclusion
\mathrm{T}M\subset\mathrm{T}M^{\mathbb{C}}
.

Many notations in the b-category agree with the corresponding notations in the Lie algebroid category. Let
\mathfrak{g}=\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}
. The subbundles
\mathcal{W}
and
\overline{\mathcal{W}}
are denoted by
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M^{1,0}}
and
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M^{0,1}}
, which are equal to
\mathfrak{g}^{1,0}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{0,1}
in the Lie algebroid category. Their dual bundles
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}^{*}M}^{1,0}
and
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}^{*}M}^{0,1}
are equal to
{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}^{1,0}
and
{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}^{0,1}
. B-forms are sections of exterior power of
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}
. The set of k-degree b-forms is denoted by
\mathcal{A}^{k}_{b}(M)
, where
\mathcal{A}^{k}_{b}(M)=\mathcal{A}^{k}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
. A similar definition for the space of sections of
(p,q)
-b-forms is
\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{b}(M)
, where
\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{b}(M)=\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
.

The definition of the b-differential,
\mathrm{d}_{b}:\mathcal{A}^{k}_{b}(M)\to\mathcal{A}^{k+1}_{b}(M)
, agrees with the Lie algebroid differential. Thus, the splitting
\mathrm{d}_{b}=\partial_{b}+\bar{\partial_{b}}
on
(p,q)
-b-forms also holds where

\displaystyle\partial_{b}:\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{b}(M)\to\mathcal{A}^{p+1,q}_{b}(M)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bar{\partial_{b}}:\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{b}(M)\to\mathcal{A}^{p,q+1}_{b}(M).

A Hermitian metric on
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}
has a canonical
(1,1)
-b-form
\omega
which is obtained by identifying
\mathcal{A}^{1,1}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
and
\mathcal{A}^{1,1}_{b}(M)
. The definition of a Kähler b-manifold is similar to the classical case and the Lie algebroid case, which is stated as follows.

Definition 16
.

A complex b-tangent bundle
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}M}
is
Kähler
if it carries a Hermitian metric whose associated canonical
(1,1)
-b-form
\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{1,1}_{b}(M)
is closed. We call
\omega
a
Kähler b-form
.

The connection in b-category

\displaystyle\prescript{b}{}{\nabla}:\Gamma(M,E)\to\mathcal{A}^{1}_{b}(M,E)

also agrees with the Lie algebroid connection. Mendoza used a b-connection to define a holomorphic vector bundle in b-category. This definition is equivalent to the definition that transition matrices of vector bundles are
\bar{\partial_{b}}
-holomorphic. Since the connections in Lie algebroid category and b-category are equivalent, we denote the Lie algebroid Chern connection by the
b-Chern connection
in the b-category. Using the b-Chern connection, we define a positive line bundle on a b-manifold as follows.

Definition 17
.

A b-holomorphic line bundle
E\to M
is
positive
if there is a Hermitian metric on
E
such that the curvature
\Theta
of the corresponding b-Chern connection satisfying that
\sqrt{-1}\Omega
is positive definite.

We now apply the Lie algebroid Kodaira–Nakano vanishing theorem to the positive line bundle over a Kähler b-manifold. Using Corollary
1
, we obtain the following result.

Proposition 5
.

Let
(M,Z)
be a Kähler b-manifold and
E\to M
be a positive line bundle. Then, the kernel of
\bar{\partial_{b}}
-Laplace

\displaystyle\Delta_{\bar{\partial_{b}}}:=\bar{\partial_{b}}\,\bar{\partial_{b}}^{*}+\bar{\partial_{b}}^{*}\,\bar{\partial_{b}}:\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{b}(M,E)\to\mathcal{A}^{p,q}_{b}(M,E)

vanishes when
p+q>n
.

Before finishing this section, we give an example of a Kähler b-manifold. Let
M
be the Riemann sphere

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}\mathrm{P}^{1}=\big\{[z_{0}:z_{1}]\big|\,(z_{0},z_{1})\neq(0,0)\big\}.

Let

U_{0}=\big\{[z_{0}:1]\big\}
and
U_{1}=\big\{[1:z_{1}]\big\}

be the Riemann sphere without the south and north poles, which naturally carry the coordinate charts maps

[z_{0}:1]\mapsto z_{0}
and

[1:z_{1}]\mapsto z_{1}
. Then,
U_{0}
and
U_{1}
form a collection of coordinate charts covering
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{P}^{1}
and their transition function on the overlap is
\phi_{0,1}:z_{0}\mapsto\frac{1}{z_{0}}
. Let
Z
be the equator of the Riemann sphere, whose precise definition is the unit circle in the coordinate charts of
U_{0}
and
U_{1}
.

We look at the b-tangent bundle
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}\mathbb{C}\mathrm{P}^{1}}
. To describe it precisely, we need to rewrite the coordinate charts
U_{0}
and
U_{1}
into polar coordinates. For
z=re^{\sqrt{-1}\theta}\neq 0
, its polar coordinate is
(r,\theta)
. Thus, we redefine the coordinate charts on the Riemann sphere without the north and south poles as follows:

\displaystyle U_{0}\backslash\{\text{south pole}\}

\displaystyle\to[0,\infty)\times[0,2\pi)

\displaystyle[z_{0}=r_{0}e^{\sqrt{-1}\theta_{0}}:1]

\displaystyle\mapsto(r_{0},\theta_{0})

and

\displaystyle U_{1}\backslash\{\text{north pole}\}

\displaystyle\to[0,\infty)\times[0,2\pi)

\displaystyle[1:z_{1}=r_{1}e^{\sqrt{-1}\theta_{1}}]

\displaystyle\mapsto(r_{1},\theta_{1}).

The coordinate transformation is:

\displaystyle\phi_{0.1}:(r_{0},\theta_{0})\mapsto(\frac{1}{r_{0}},-\theta_{0}).

These coordinates naturally induce
\{\frac{\partial}{\partial r_{0}},\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{0}}\}
and
\{\frac{\partial}{\partial r_{1}},\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{1}}\}
as the frame of tangent bundle over
U_{0}\backslash\{\text{south pole}\}
and
U_{1}\backslash\{\text{north pole}\}
.

We define a smooth function
f(r)
on
(0,\infty)
with constant value 1 on
(0,\frac{1}{2}]
and
[2,\infty)
, and 0 at
r=1
. Moreover, we require
f(r)=f(\frac{1}{r})
. Using
f(r)
, we define
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}\mathbb{C}\mathrm{P}^{1}}
on
U_{0}\backslash\{\text{north pole}\}
as the span of the frame
f(r)\frac{\partial}{\partial r_{0}}
and
\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{0}}
, and use
R_{0}
and
\Theta_{0}
to denote these frames with the anchor maps

\displaystyle\rho(R_{0})=f(r_{0})\frac{\partial}{\partial r_{0}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\rho(\Theta_{0})=\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{0}}.

The definition of
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}\mathbb{C}\mathrm{P}^{1}}
on
U_{1}\backslash\{\text{north pole}\}
is similar as it is spanned by the frame
R_{1}
and
\Theta_{1}
, where
\rho(R_{1})=f(r_{1})\frac{\partial}{\partial r_{1}}

and

\rho(\Theta_{1})=\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{1}}
. The assumption that
f(r)=f(\frac{1}{r})
means that
R_{0},\Theta_{0}
and
R_{1}\Theta_{1}
are well defined.

The complex structure of
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}\mathbb{C}\mathrm{P}^{1}}
is formally the same as the tangent bundle of
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{P}^{1}
. The almost complex structure on
\mathrm{T}\mathbb{C}\mathrm{P}^{1}
is

\displaystyle J\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial r_{0}}\bigg|_{(r_{0},\theta_{0})}\big)=\frac{1}{r_{0}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{0}}\bigg|_{(r_{0},\theta_{0})}\qquad\text{and}\qquad J\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{0}}\bigg|_{(r_{0},\theta_{0})}\big)=-r_{0}\frac{\partial}{\partial r_{0}}\bigg|_{(r_{0},\theta_{0})}

on
U_{0}\backslash\{\text{south pole}\}
and the same on
U_{1}\backslash\{\text{north pole}\}
. Following this idea, we define the almost complex structure of
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}\mathbb{C}\mathrm{P}^{1}}
, which is

\displaystyle J\big(R_{0}\big|_{(r_{0},\theta_{0})}\big)=\frac{1}{r_{0}}\Theta_{0}\big|_{(r_{0},\theta_{0})}\qquad\text{and}\qquad J\big(\Theta_{0}\big|_{(r_{0},\theta_{0})}\big)=-r_{0}R_{0}\big|_{(r_{0},\theta_{0})}

on
U_{0}\backslash\{\text{south pole}\}
and the same on
U_{1}\backslash\{\text{north pole}\}
. We can easily check that
J
is well defined under the assumption that
f(r)=f(\frac{1}{r})
.

Recall the Fubini–Study form on
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{P}^{1}
, which is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\frac{1}{1+z_{0}\bar{z_{0}}}dz_{0}\wedge d\bar{z_{0}}=\frac{1}{(1+r_{0}^{2})^{2}}d\theta_{0}\wedge dr_{0}

on the coordinate chart
U_{0}
and the same on the coordinate
U_{1}
. To generalize this to
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}\mathbb{C}\mathrm{P}^{1}}
, we first define the frame
R_{0}^{*},\Theta_{0}^{*}
for the dual bundle
\prescript{b}{}{\mathrm{T}\mathbb{C}\mathrm{P}^{1}}^{*}
on
U_{0}\backslash\{\text{south pole}\}
by setting

\displaystyle R_{0}^{*}(R_{0})=1,\quad\quad R_{0}^{*}(\Theta_{0})=0,\quad\quad\Theta_{0}^{*}(R_{0})=0,\quad\text{and}\quad\Theta_{0}^{*}(\Theta_{0})=1.

The same definition is applied to
R_{1}^{*},\Theta_{1}^{*}
on
U_{1}\backslash\{\text{north pole}\}
. Then, we construct a b-tangent Kähler form
\prescript{b}{}{\omega}
which is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1+r_{0}^{2})^{2}}\Theta_{0}^{*}\wedge R_{0}^{*}

on
U_{0}\backslash\{\text{south pole}\}
and the same on
U_{1}\backslash\{\text{north pole}\}
. It is easy to check that this is well defined: it is a closed form since it is a top degree form.

Everything we defined above is naturally extended to the two poles since
R_{0},\Theta_{0}
, and
R_{1},\Theta_{1}
are in total agreement with
\frac{\partial}{\partial r_{0}},\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{0}}
and
\frac{\partial}{\partial r_{1}},\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{1}}
on the region that the radial coordinate is in:
(0,\frac{1}{2})
and
(2,\infty)
.

4
Appendix I

Lemma 6
.

The equations

\displaystyle[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](\xi_{\alpha}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(\xi_{\alpha}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

(46)

and

\displaystyle[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\alpha}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\alpha}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

(47)

hold.

It is enough to verify (
46
) since (
47
) is equal to the conjugation of (
46
). We prove the lemma by induction. Our proof is based on the assumption that the equation

\displaystyle[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})=\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}(\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})

holds from the induction condition. We separate the proof of Lemma
6
into four parts:

1.

Calculate
[\bar{\partial}_{\mathfrak{g}},\iota_{\omega}](\xi_{\alpha}\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}})
in Section
4.1
.

2.

Calculate
(\sqrt{-1}\partial_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\alpha})\wedge\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\wedge\xi^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{J}^{{}^{\prime}}}
in Section
4.2
.

3.

Calculate
